commit 12b56ab8577712afe143bb10eba3d92e92fb8a08
Merge: 55d993a7 3d3d56c8

merge: INPUT-ROW-LIMIT (fix/p5-input-row-limit @ 3d3d56c8) into train 12

P5 train 12, merge 4 of 4. fx v0.0.16's `inputRowLimit` for every surface
that reads it: the composer's row cap, every menu's page and the `@` list's
PgUp/PgDn page. Base 32afd307.

Conflicts, as forecast:
- scripts/fxcap/parity-ledger.toml, six diff3 blocks, each the same
  `src/tui/shell.rs:N` moved by both sides (main's tests and this unit's
  233 lines of tests): train 11's blocks.py, ours' numbers kept; the
  re-point commit after this merge moves every reference the merges moved
  to the head's lines.
- docs/parity.md, two blocks (train 12's rowsmerge.py, rowmerge.py's token
  rule for a block of several rows): the `/model` row main's alone, the
  `/effort` row the unit's alone, the full-screen TUI row token by token
  (main's 88 edits and the unit's 8, none overlapping or meeting).

One semantic conflict, not forecast: the tree did not build (E0308). The
unit's `at_41_rows_a_page_of_the_model_menu_is_19_models` gives
`CatalogLoaded` an `Instant` due, which CATALOG-REFRESH (train 11, on main)
made a `CatalogDue`; spelt as every other test of the module spells a
load's due (train 12's semantic.py). The merge tree then passed cargo check
(both feature sets), cargo fmt --check and clippy (both), and the unit's 11
cases pass on it.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

While a failure's hold holds -- by the readings above -- `/model` opens the menu over the failure at once and asks nothing; past it the menu says `Loading models…` while it reads again behind the failure (`refreshMenuLocked`, `:1046-1050`). **A turn whose request is planned from the catalog** -- a model whose window nothing else gives, a level, Fast or Ultra, the prompt's own images (`requiresResolvedRequestCapabilities`, `orchestrator.zig:5658-5670`, at the turn's start, `:5884-5893`) -- waits two seconds at most for the retry behind a failed catalog that is on its way, such as the one its prompt started, and goes without it after them (`resolveForRequest`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:843-866`, `failed_catalog_retry_wait_ms`, `:45-47`); and once a turn more, at the first request carrying images -- the prompt's, the history's or a tool result's -- while the model is still unknown, the image route asks again and waits as long (`onRequest`, `src/core/images/image_route.zig:180-184`; ResolveOncePerTurn, `orchestrator.zig:7502-7509`). No other request waits. **A stop during the turn's own wait** -- a first load's or a retry's, for the window, a level, a speed or the prompt's own images, which wait for a first load at the turn's start whatever the window (`orchestrator.zig:5885-5886`) -- ends the turn as upstream's does: interrupted, the stop's one `Cancelled` line and no error, its prompt and images kept as asked and stopped, and no refusal said for what the catalog could not tell, Ultra's or an image's (`resolveForRequest`'s cancel, `model_cache_runtime.zig:855-865`; `orchestrator.zig:5893-5919`, and the check after the wait, `:5923-5948`, ahead of Ultra's refusal at the request, `:7657`). **One in the image route's wait at the first request is not copied**: upstream's `try` there (`orchestrator.zig:7508`) ends the turn failed, keeps nothing (`:6092-6134`; `finishExecutionOnlyFailureIfNeeded`, `finalization.zig:226-241`) and says `request failed: Cancelled` under `system` (`:5616-5626`; `app_process_runtime.zig:157-169`, admitted after the stop, `app_worker_runtime.zig:386`); xfx keeps nothing either and ends it as the stop, without the line. **The TUI reads the catalog in the background at launch and after a provider switch**, as upstream warms its model cache (`startModelCacheWarmup`, `src/main.zig:2007-2041@481104fd`, from `app_entry_runtime.zig:340` and `app_auth_runtime.zig:2015-2024`): its answer -- like every read's -- reaches the band the moment it arrives, a turn running or not, as upstream's interface takes a load's end on its next tick (`pollLoadTransition`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:745-760`, from `src/main.zig:2878-2880`); a turn that needs it waits for it. A read is stopped and waited for when a provider switch replaces it, so no old provider's rows follow the switch. **The catalogs**: a llmux daemon's `GET /models`, and the Gateway's `GET {origin}/coding-agent/v1/models` -- the origin of the completion endpoint, so the key goes nowhere a turn does not -- with the turn's bearer, no redirect followed, **read whole or not at all** (CATALOG-BOUNDS): within fx's 30 s, over the whole request from connect to the last byte, and within fx's 16 MiB -- a cap on the body's length as it is read off the wire, an answer of exactly 16 MiB read; not a ceiling on memory: the buffer can grow to just under twice that (`Vec` doubling), and the parsed document (`serde_json::Value`) is a multiple of it, as fx too parses the whole answer (`gateway_json_fetch_timeout_ms`, `max_gateway_json_bytes`, `src/gateway/client.zig:486-509,597-635@481104fd`). Reaching the deadline -- connecting, waiting for the head, or mid-body -- is a failure fx asks again about, and the menu says `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.`; an answer past 16 MiB, declared or grown into, is refused rather than cut to fit, a malformed answer (`catalogRequestFailure`, `src/builtins/gateway.zig:2510-2533@481104fd`), so the menu says `Unable to load models.` and, like any failed catalog, it is asked again only at the next ask past its hold, as above; a body that stops partway, or is not UTF-8, is no catalog either. xfx gives the connect alone 10 s inside the 30 (fx gives it the whole 30): either way a retried failure, in the same words. A request that got no answer at all -- refused, reset, a name that does not resolve -- xfx also says it retries, where fx asks again only after its deadline, a reset or a closing connection (`isRetryableGatewayError`, `src/gateway/client.zig:19-23@481104fd`) and says `Unable to load models.` for the rest, a refused connection among them: MODELS-REASK's choice, unchanged here. **The llmux daemon's catalog has bounds of xfx's own** -- 3 s to connect, 5 s in all, 1 MiB, read the same way, whole or not at all -- because fx has no llmux and the daemon is on this machine; an answer past 1 MiB or not UTF-8 is a malformed catalog (`check the daemon is an llmux version compatible with xfx`), and one that never came whole -- no connection, a failed status (404, 503), a body cut short -- is an unavailable one (`check the daemon is running`); `xfx setup llmux`'s two probes read the same way. parsed for upstream's fields (language rows only; id, vendor, release, `tool-use`, efforts, context window, output ceiling, and Fast/Ultra from `fast_options` or the priced service tiers). A Gateway with no credential has no catalog: upstream falls back to the public one, and xfx does not contact a remote host it was given no key for. With one word it is upstream's router form: the runtime resolves it against the catalog -- loading it first when none is loaded -- an id or an alias spelled exactly, else upstream's fuzzy score (substring, every word, letters in order, any word) over the ids in menu order -- the router's own, which v0.0.16 kept as c1db919 had it (`fuzzyModelScore` and `resolveModelQueryFromIds`, `session_commands.zig:1425-1448,1760@481104fd`, unchanged) while the menu and the column moved to the ranks above -- else the word as typed, **which is applied rather than refused**, as upstream applies an id its catalog does not list. When the band holds the catalog it resolves the word itself, by the same rule (`provider::model::resolve_in_order`), and sends the id -- so the change is announced the moment it is given, as upstream's is; with no catalog in hand the word goes as typed and the runtime's answer is the announcement. **A resolved id is held to the typed word's rule** (`provider::model::model_id_problem`: one word, at most 200 bytes, no control character, C1 included): a catalog id that breaks it is refused, not adopted -- by the runtime, and by the band before it announces an id it resolved or a row it picked -- and the refusal is xfx's own words, never the id. So is every model the profile holds: a settings `model` or `models.<provider>` that breaks the rule is dropped at load with the `invalid_value` diagnostic, and no writer puts one there (`/model`, a speed save, `xfx setup` and `/setup`, `/provider`), `xfx setup llmux` passing over a catalog entry whose name breaks it and refusing a catalog with none that keeps it. Upstream adopts a resolved id (`setResolvedModelRuntime`, `session_commands.zig:1256-1260` at `c1db9191`, `:1399` at `481104fd`) and refuses only the writes that would persist it -- the session's (`validateModel`, `session_codec.zig:3165` at `c1db9191`, `:3830` at `481104fd`) and the settings' (`validateUserPatch`, `putModelPreference`, `settings_store.zig:921,1633`) -- and refuses such a model when it reads settings (`config_runtime.zig:1645,1674`); xfx refuses it at adoption as well, which is unsafe to copy, because the catalog is the provider's document, its parser takes any non-empty id, and an id adopted is announced and painted at once. **The inline column** (`input_completion_runtime.zig:1123-1295`): `/model ` and a space open the catalog's models under the argument -- from the column the argument starts at, six rows at most, the marked row in the bold hint grey and the rest dim -- and the text after it searches them as the menu does, the same ranks in the catalog's order and the first 32 of them (`modelCompletions`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:895-913@481104fd`), so `/model gpt6` lists `openai/gpt-6-astra`, the mark starting on the first ranked row -- and Tab, which writes the marked row, puts it back there, as fx's replacement does (`composer_text_replacement.zig:73-92`), since the line written can list fuzzy matches under its own row; a space typed after a query that is not a model's exact id is not inserted, as in fx (the Space branch, `app_input_runtime.zig:1355-1363@481104fd`, inserts nothing when `advanceModelPickerOnSpace`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:1210-1219`, finds no exact id), so a query of several words reaches the column pasted; Up and Down wrap, Tab writes the marked row into the composer, Escape puts the column away until the line stops being a `/model` line, and Enter on a model with reasoning levels writes `/model <id> ` and opens the **effort column** under the next token: `default`, then the catalog's levels in its order, filtered by what is typed, Enter taking the level spelled out or the marked one -- and, for a model whose row offers Fast, writing `/model <id> <level> ` and opening the **speed column** under the next token (`normal`, then `fast` and `ultrafast` where the row offers them, `fast` marked), whose Enter makes the change; for a model without Fast the level's Enter makes the change, keeping the session's Fast only as far as the row offers it -- so a model that offers Ultra and not Fast cannot be given Ultra from the column, as upstream's cannot (`submitModelPicker`, `:1183`). fx v0.0.16 opens the same effort column on its own for an `/effort ` line, over the model in force and with no column before it (the `/effort` row). A model with no levels and a speed opens the speed column at once (`/model <id> auto `); one with neither is applied from the model column at once. The speed column marks a speed rather than a row (a typed speed resets it to `normal`), Tab writes all three tokens, Left goes back to the levels with the session's own speed marked, and a level spelled out and a space opens it for a model with either speed. Left steps back a column. A space at the column is the column's: after an id spelled exactly it is Enter -- so a typed `/model <id> <more>` for a model with no levels and no speed makes the change and sends the rest as a prompt, as upstream's does -- after a level spelled exactly it opens the speed column for a model with a speed, and otherwise it does nothing. Typing or a Backspace at the end of the effort column keeps it; any other edit, a paste included, goes back to the models. Without rows the column says `loading models...` (and asks the runtime for them, as a control message, whatever the queue holds), `unable to load models` -- and the next frame that draws it asks again once the failure's hold (above) is over, as upstream's render path lists the column's models through `ensureModelCache` (`app_render_runtime.zig:559-568@481104fd`, `src/main.zig:2167-2169`), saying `unable to load models` still while that read is on its way, as upstream's column reads the cache's state, which a retry leaves `.failed` (`app_render_runtime.zig:752-753`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:633-634`), so the column turns into the rows when a read succeeds; an idle session paints no frame and asks nothing, as upstream's does -- `no matching models`, `no matching effort` or `no matching mode`; while a model is pending the hint row previews it, the marked level and the marked speed -- `⚡︎` for Fast from the moment the levels open on a model that offers it, as upstream's does. Enter on a menu row goes on into the effort column for a model with levels (`beginExactModelSelection`) and applies one without. **The explicit form** `/model <id> <effort>`: a literal id, never fuzzy-resolved, and an effort the catalog lists for it or `auto`/`default`; it is applied with that effort and announced; a third word is the speed -- `fast` or `ultrafast` where the row offers it, `normal` where it offers either to decline -- and two words mean `normal`; anything else -- four words, an unlisted effort, a speed the row does not offer -- is upstream's `✗ usage: /model <id> <effort> [normal|fast|ultrafast]`, with the composer kept. **The reasoning effort** is one per session, carried across model changes, saved as `effort` in `~/.xfx/settings.json` (profile-only, `auto` for the default) beside `models.<provider>` in the same write, and -- by the TUI; `xfx ask` and the line-oriented shell read the key but send no effort yet -- sent with every request to a model whose catalog row lists it, the catalog read before the first such turn when nothing has read it: the Gateway's v4 route as a top-level `"reasoning"` (`max` sent as `xhigh`, the route's top tier), llmux as `output_config.effort`, the field every llmux route reads it from; a model the catalog does not describe is sent none. **The speed** -- Fast and Ultra, exclusive, both off whenever the model changes by a query, and held to what the row offers by a pick -- is saved as `fast_mode`, `ultrafast_mode` and `fast_mode_model_bound` (profile-only) beside every model and speed change, and sent by the TUI on the Gateway as upstream's `providerOptions`: `{"gateway":{"speed":"fast"}}` for Fast on a row that offers it, `{"gateway":{"only":["openai"]},"openai":{"serviceTier":"ultrafast"}}` for Ultra -- which, with no silent downgrade, refuses the turn unless the provider is the Gateway, the id is `openai/…` and the row offers it. **llmux reads no per-request speed** (its Fast is the daemon's own codex setting, `2lab-ai/llmux src/config/schema.rs:545-550`, and its catalog publishes no speed), so no llmux row offers one, the speed column never opens there, and nothing is sent. The announcement is upstream's `* Switched to <id> (effort: <level or default>, speed: <normal|fast|ultrafast>)` -- `fast` also for an id ending `-fast` -- or `* Next turn will use …` while a turn runs or waits; the hint row's compact label follows the model in force, with the level after it when the model takes it, then `⚡︎` -- in the row's grey for Fast (bound to the model, or an id ending `-fast`), in upstream's yellow (`38;2;255;204;0`, `38;5;220` at 256 colours) for Ultra. **A change is a control message**, never refused for a full queue: while a turn runs -- or a permission or model question waits, which it does not answer -- it is put aside and applied the moment that turn ends, before anything queued behind it. One put aside while `/provider` switched is saved for the provider it was chosen under and lands on no selector of the new one (upstream refuses the switch while work runs, so its end state is the same), with a warning that says so. A failed catalog load says upstream's `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.` (or `AI Gateway rate limited model discovery; xfx retries automatically.`) where asking again could help -- upstream's words since 14a12fea, its own name read as xfx's -- and `Unable to load models.` otherwise (`retryableFailureText`, `model_menu_presentation.zig:435-443@481104fd`); either is read again past its hold, as above. Upstream's third retryable sentence, `Could not refresh model catalog; xfx retries automatically.` in xfx's name, is for any other failure asking again could change: a catalog read that could be given no thread, or no runtime to run on (upstream's `.runtime`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:537-546`), told the moment it is found, a turn running or not, and read again past its hold like any other. A read past its deadline is upstream's retryable `.transport` (`catalogRequestFailure`, `builtins/gateway.zig:2510-2519`, its test `:2545-2547`) -- `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.` -- which xfx says for a read whose send times out, or whose body is still coming at the deadline (`GatewayCatalog::fetch`, CATALOG-BOUNDS). The choice is saved as `models.<provider>` in `~/.xfx/settings.json` -- with `provider`, the speed keys and, for a pick (a menu row, the column, the explicit form, a `C-p` row, `/settings`' model list) of a model whose row lists levels, `effort` -- the one it chose or the session's -- as upstream's `selectModelFromPicker` writes them (`session_commands.zig:886-915`; a query writes no effort, `setResolvedModel`, `:1172-1188`) -- on the runtime thread, through the profile's staged `0600` writer, every other key kept, and read back at the next launch under the usual precedence (`XFX_MODEL` over the profile over the compiled default); when a higher layer will still decide a fresh session's model it says `* model: saved to user settings (scope=user); fresh sessions here use higher-precedence model=<layer>` -- `model picker:` for a change made in a column, the menu or the explicit form -- and a failed write `✗ model: active for this process but not saved to user settings (…)`. Every settings write -- a preference, and `xfx setup`'s, which merges the selection it decided into the file as it stands under the lock -- is one read-modify-write under an in-process lock and upstream's cross-process `settings.lock` beside the file, so `/statusline`, `/model` and a setup writing at once all survive. A durable `preferences_changed` event records the model, and the effort and both switches with every change to them -- and at the session's first save, which is the first prompt or a `/rename` before it, the ones in force, right behind `session_started`: a change made before then had no log to go into, and upstream holds every setting made before the first turn and writes them with it (`hold`, `writeFirstTurn`, `src/core/session_manager/session.zig:415-429`, `:503-539@481104fd`) -- and a resume -- at launch or from the picker -- puts the session's effort and speed back, as upstream's `restoreRuntimePreferences` does. **The line-oriented shell** keeps its own report: bare, the model, the provider and the layer that chose it, and the provider's catalog in the order it was published, bounded at 100 rows with an explicit `... and N more` line; with an argument the same resolution against a catalog it has loaded (an id it does not list is applied, not refused), the shared caveat (`provider::model::unverified_notice`) when no catalog could be consulted, and no profile write. **What a model id may be is one question with one answer on both surfaces**: `provider::model::model_id_problem` -- one printable word of at most 200 bytes, because the id becomes an HTTP header and a session-log field -- asked before the runtime is told anything. Upstream's Ctrl-P model picker is not here. The standalone `models` and `provider` commands remain separate deferred rows. `commands.zig:452`, `model_menu_presentation.zig`, `session_commands.zig:1147-1279`. |
-| `/effort` | slash | implemented | fx v0.0.16's command (`vercel-labs/fx@481104fd`, commit `04a5e3af`): upstream's row, "choose the reasoning effort for the current model", Model, after `/model` (`commands.zig:459`). **The TUI** answers it as upstream does. The current model's catalog row is read first, whatever follows the name: a model whose row lists no level, or a catalog this side does not hold, says `* effort: <model> has no levels to choose from` -- or, while the catalog read is still on its way, `* effort: levels for <model> are still loading; try /effort again in a moment` -- and nothing else happens; nothing is asked of the runtime (`handleEffort`, `session_commands.zig:1000-1013`). Bare, it opens the Effort menu in the band under the composer and writes nothing to the document: `Effort N` in the bold hint grey and the model in the dim grey (its middle given up when it does not fit), a blank row, then `default` and the model's levels in the row's order, the marked one bold and the rest dim, `current` in the dim grey two cells past the widest label on the level in use; it opens on the level in use, or on `default` for one the model does not offer (`effort_menu.zig:30-49`, `effort_menu_presentation.zig`). Up and Down (and `C-k`/`C-j`) **wrap**; Page Up/Down move by the composer's row cap and wrap the same way (upstream's page keys there are uncaptured); Enter closes it and applies the marked level; Escape closes it; any edit to the composer -- a keystroke, a paste -- closes it and the edit stands (`app_input_runtime.zig:1984-1990`, `:2869-2884`). Shift-Tab is not the menu's: it cycles the permission mode with the menu up, and the menu stays, as upstream's (`.toggle_permission_mode`, `app_input_runtime.zig:1048-1057`). A model change, a provider switch or a resume that lands while the menu is up closes it, so Enter cannot apply one model's level to another; upstream has no such rule, because its changes are commands and typing one closes the menu. Its key help is `↑↓ navigate     enter use     esc close` and upstream's narrower variants, or `press ctrl+c again to exit` while a Ctrl-C would leave (`input_presentation.zig:671-702`). A short footer gives up the blank row, then the title, before a level, and the levels then scroll with the mark (`effort_menu_presentation.zig:17-59`; the end of the conversation stays in view). With a word, `/effort <level>` applies a level the row lists, spelled as it lists it, or `default`/`auto`/`adaptive` in any case; a well-formed name it does not list says `* effort: <name> is not available for <model>; levels: default, …`, and anything that is not a level name says `* effort: levels for <model>: default, …` without repeating it (`session_commands.zig:1014-1037`). For a model with no levels the line shows no column at all, and Enter runs the command. The column reads the catalog it has and asks for none. `loadedCatalogStatusText`, `model_menu_presentation.zig:403-404`). **When the catalog is read again** is upstream's model cache's rule (`beginLoad`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:570-648`), and every trigger is the user's own activity, so an idle session never reads it (`:27-28`): the launch and a provider switch read it; the menu opening reads a catalog that failed, and rows five seconds after the last read began; a submitted prompt or steer -- asked as it is submitted, ahead of the work, even behind a running turn (`noteModelCatalogActivity`, `src/main.zig:1201`, `:1225-1232`) -- and a frame that draws the inline column read a catalog that failed, and rows five minutes after the read that brought them began (the Gateway's `refresh_interval_ms`, `builtins/gateway.zig:2348`, counted from `last_attempt_ms`, set as a read starts, `:609-611`, `:636`; llmux, which upstream has not, is held to the same) or once a read behind them failed in a way asking again could change; one it could not change leaves them to the five minutes, counted from that read's start, and leaves the menu's note as it was (`loadedCatalogStatusText` then reads the loaded catalog's provenance, `model_menu_presentation.zig:405-433`). The runtime tells the band, with every answer, when its clock next owes the catalog a read, and the band asks then. **After a failure, nothing reads it for 5 s, then 10 s, 20 s and so on up to five minutes after each failure in a row**, whatever asks, counted from when the failure arrived; a success clears the count (`failureBackoffMs`, `:40-54`; `completeFetch`, `:936-958`); a provider switch starts over. **The clock is one that stops while the machine sleeps** (Rust's `Instant`: `CLOCK_UPTIME_RAW` on macOS, `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` on Linux), where fx's catalog clock keeps counting through sleep (`clockMs`, `std.Io.Clock.boot`, `src/core/gateway/model_catalog.zig:72-79@481104fd`): both ignore wall-clock changes, but after a laptop sleep fx's catalog is as old as the sleep made it, due again on the next ask, and xfx's is only as old as the time the machine was awake -- and a backoff likewise. Not yet copied (CATALOG-BOUNDS found it; a clock that counts sleep would replace `Instant` across the catalog's state). Inside a failure's hold `/model` opens the menu over the failure at once and asks nothing; past it the menu says `Loading models…` while it reads again behind the failure (`refreshMenuLocked`, `:1046-1050`). **A turn whose request is planned from the catalog** -- a model whose window nothing else gives, a level, Fast or Ultra, the prompt's own images (`requiresResolvedRequestCapabilities`, `orchestrator.zig:5658-5670`, at the turn's start, `:5884-5893`) -- waits two seconds at most for the retry behind a failed catalog that is on its way, such as the one its prompt started, and goes without it after them (`resolveForRequest`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:843-866`, `failed_catalog_retry_wait_ms`, `:45-47`); and once a turn more, at the first request carrying images -- the prompt's, the history's or a tool result's -- while the model is still unknown, the image route asks again and waits as long (`onRequest`, `src/core/images/image_route.zig:180-184`; ResolveOncePerTurn, `orchestrator.zig:7502-7509`). No other request waits. **A stop during the turn's own wait** -- a first load's or a retry's, for the window, a level, a speed or the prompt's own images, which wait for a first load at the turn's start whatever the window (`orchestrator.zig:5885-5886`) -- ends the turn as upstream's does: interrupted, the stop's one `Cancelled` line and no error, its prompt and images kept as asked and stopped, and no refusal said for what the catalog could not tell, Ultra's or an image's (`resolveForRequest`'s cancel, `model_cache_runtime.zig:855-865`; `orchestrator.zig:5893-5919`, and the check after the wait, `:5923-5948`, ahead of Ultra's refusal at the request, `:7657`). **One in the image route's wait at the first request is not copied**: upstream's `try` there (`orchestrator.zig:7508`) ends the turn failed, keeps nothing (`:6092-6134`; `finishExecutionOnlyFailureIfNeeded`, `finalization.zig:226-241`) and says `request failed: Cancelled` under `system` (`:5616-5626`; `app_process_runtime.zig:157-169`, admitted after the stop, `app_worker_runtime.zig:386`); xfx keeps nothing either and ends it as the stop, without the line. **The TUI reads the catalog in the background at launch and after a provider switch**, as upstream warms its model cache (`startModelCacheWarmup`, `src/main.zig:2007-2041@481104fd`, from `app_entry_runtime.zig:340` and `app_auth_runtime.zig:2015-2024`): its answer -- like every read's -- reaches the band the moment it arrives, a turn running or not, as upstream's interface takes a load's end on its next tick (`pollLoadTransition`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:745-760`, from `src/main.zig:2878-2880`); a turn that needs it waits for it. A read is stopped and waited for when a provider switch replaces it, so no old provider's rows follow the switch. **The catalogs**: a llmux daemon's `GET /models`, and the Gateway's `GET {origin}/coding-agent/v1/models` -- the origin of the completion endpoint, so the key goes nowhere a turn does not -- with the turn's bearer, no redirect followed, **read whole or not at all** (CATALOG-BOUNDS): within fx's 30 s, over the whole request from connect to the last byte, and within fx's 16 MiB -- a cap on the body's length as it is read off the wire, an answer of exactly 16 MiB read; not a ceiling on memory: the buffer can grow to just under twice that (`Vec` doubling), and the parsed document (`serde_json::Value`) is a multiple of it, as fx too parses the whole answer (`gateway_json_fetch_timeout_ms`, `max_gateway_json_bytes`, `src/gateway/client.zig:486-509,597-635@481104fd`). Reaching the deadline -- connecting, waiting for the head, or mid-body -- is a failure fx asks again about, and the menu says `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.`; an answer past 16 MiB, declared or grown into, is refused rather than cut to fit, a malformed answer (`catalogRequestFailure`, `src/builtins/gateway.zig:2510-2533@481104fd`), so the menu says `Unable to load models.` and, like any failed catalog, it is asked again only at the next ask after the backoff; a body that stops partway, or is not UTF-8, is no catalog either. xfx gives the connect alone 10 s inside the 30 (fx gives it the whole 30): either way a retried failure, in the same words. A request that got no answer at all -- refused, reset, a name that does not resolve -- xfx also says it retries, where fx asks again only after its deadline, a reset or a closing connection (`isRetryableGatewayError`, `src/gateway/client.zig:19-23@481104fd`) and says `Unable to load models.` for the rest, a refused connection among them: MODELS-REASK's choice, unchanged here. **The llmux daemon's catalog has bounds of xfx's own** -- 3 s to connect, 5 s in all, 1 MiB, read the same way, whole or not at all -- because fx has no llmux and the daemon is on this machine; an answer past 1 MiB or not UTF-8 is a malformed catalog (`check the daemon is an llmux version compatible with xfx`), and one that never came whole -- no connection, a failed status (404, 503), a body cut short -- is an unavailable one (`check the daemon is running`); `xfx setup llmux`'s two probes read the same way. parsed for upstream's fields (language rows only; id, vendor, release, `tool-use`, efforts, context window, output ceiling, and Fast/Ultra from `fast_options` or the priced service tiers). A Gateway with no credential has no catalog: upstream falls back to the public one, and xfx does not contact a remote host it was given no key for. With one word it is upstream's router form: the runtime resolves it against the catalog -- loading it first when none is loaded -- an id or an alias spelled exactly, else upstream's fuzzy score (substring, every word, letters in order, any word) over the ids in menu order -- the router's own, which v0.0.16 kept as c1db919 had it (`fuzzyModelScore` and `resolveModelQueryFromIds`, `session_commands.zig:1425-1448,1760@481104fd`, unchanged) while the menu and the column moved to the ranks above -- else the word as typed, **which is applied rather than refused**, as upstream applies an id its catalog does not list. When the band holds the catalog it resolves the word itself, by the same rule (`provider::model::resolve_in_order`), and sends the id -- so the change is announced the moment it is given, as upstream's is; with no catalog in hand the word goes as typed and the runtime's answer is the announcement. **A resolved id is held to the typed word's rule** (`provider::model::model_id_problem`: one word, at most 200 bytes, no control character, C1 included): a catalog id that breaks it is refused, not adopted -- by the runtime, and by the band before it announces an id it resolved or a row it picked -- and the refusal is xfx's own words, never the id. So is every model the profile holds: a settings `model` or `models.<provider>` that breaks the rule is dropped at load with the `invalid_value` diagnostic, and no writer puts one there (`/model`, a speed save, `xfx setup` and `/setup`, `/provider`), `xfx setup llmux` passing over a catalog entry whose name breaks it and refusing a catalog with none that keeps it. Upstream adopts a resolved id (`setResolvedModelRuntime`, `session_commands.zig:1256-1260` at `c1db9191`, `:1399` at `481104fd`) and refuses only the writes that would persist it -- the session's (`validateModel`, `session_codec.zig:3165` at `c1db9191`, `:3830` at `481104fd`) and the settings' (`validateUserPatch`, `putModelPreference`, `settings_store.zig:921,1633`) -- and refuses such a model when it reads settings (`config_runtime.zig:1645,1674`); xfx refuses it at adoption as well, which is unsafe to copy, because the catalog is the provider's document, its parser takes any non-empty id, and an id adopted is announced and painted at once. **The inline column** (`input_completion_runtime.zig:1123-1295`): `/model ` and a space open the catalog's models under the argument -- from the column the argument starts at, six rows at most, the marked row in the bold hint grey and the rest dim -- and the text after it searches them as the menu does, the same ranks in the catalog's order and the first 32 of them (`modelCompletions`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:895-913@481104fd`), so `/model gpt6` lists `openai/gpt-6-astra`, the mark starting on the first ranked row -- and Tab, which writes the marked row, puts it back there, as fx's replacement does (`composer_text_replacement.zig:73-92`), since the line written can list fuzzy matches under its own row; a space typed after a query that is not a model's exact id is not inserted, as in fx (the Space branch, `app_input_runtime.zig:1355-1363@481104fd`, inserts nothing when `advanceModelPickerOnSpace`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:1210-1219`, finds no exact id), so a query of several words reaches the column pasted; Up and Down wrap, Tab writes the marked row into the composer, Escape puts the column away until the line stops being a `/model` line, and Enter on a model with reasoning levels writes `/model <id> ` and opens the **effort column** under the next token: `default`, then the catalog's levels in its order, filtered by what is typed, Enter taking the level spelled out or the marked one -- and, for a model whose row offers Fast, writing `/model <id> <level> ` and opening the **speed column** under the next token (`normal`, then `fast` and `ultrafast` where the row offers them, `fast` marked), whose Enter makes the change; for a model without Fast the level's Enter makes the change, keeping the session's Fast only as far as the row offers it -- so a model that offers Ultra and not Fast cannot be given Ultra from the column, as upstream's cannot (`submitModelPicker`, `:1183`). fx v0.0.16 opens the same effort column on its own for an `/effort ` line, over the model in force and with no column before it (the `/effort` row). A model with no levels and a speed opens the speed column at once (`/model <id> auto `); one with neither is applied from the model column at once. The speed column marks a speed rather than a row (a typed speed resets it to `normal`), Tab writes all three tokens, Left goes back to the levels with the session's own speed marked, and a level spelled out and a space opens it for a model with either speed. Left steps back a column. A space at the column is the column's: after an id spelled exactly it is Enter -- so a typed `/model <id> <more>` for a model with no levels and no speed makes the change and sends the rest as a prompt, as upstream's does -- after a level spelled exactly it opens the speed column for a model with a speed, and otherwise it does nothing. Typing or a Backspace at the end of the effort column keeps it; any other edit, a paste included, goes back to the models. Without rows the column says `loading models...` (and asks the runtime for them, as a control message, whatever the queue holds), `unable to load models` -- and the next frame that draws it asks again once the failure's hold is over, as upstream's render path lists the column's models through `ensureModelCache` (`app_render_runtime.zig:559-568@481104fd`, `src/main.zig:2167-2169`), saying `unable to load models` still while that read is on its way, as upstream's column reads the cache's state, which a retry leaves `.failed` (`app_render_runtime.zig:752-753`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:633-634`), so the column turns into the rows when a read succeeds; an idle session paints no frame and asks nothing, as upstream's does -- `no matching models`, `no matching effort` or `no matching mode`; while a model is pending the hint row previews it, the marked level and the marked speed -- `⚡︎` for Fast from the moment the levels open on a model that offers it, as upstream's does. Enter on a menu row goes on into the effort column for a model with levels (`beginExactModelSelection`) and applies one without. **The explicit form** `/model <id> <effort>`: a literal id, never fuzzy-resolved, and an effort the catalog lists for it or `auto`/`default`; it is applied with that effort and announced; a third word is the speed -- `fast` or `ultrafast` where the row offers it, `normal` where it offers either to decline -- and two words mean `normal`; anything else -- four words, an unlisted effort, a speed the row does not offer -- is upstream's `✗ usage: /model <id> <effort> [normal|fast|ultrafast]`, with the composer kept. **The reasoning effort** is one per session, carried across model changes, saved as `effort` in `~/.xfx/settings.json` (profile-only, `auto` for the default) beside `models.<provider>` in the same write, and -- by the TUI; `xfx ask` and the line-oriented shell read the key but send no effort yet -- sent with every request to a model whose catalog row lists it, the catalog read before the first such turn when nothing has read it: the Gateway's v4 route as a top-level `"reasoning"` (`max` sent as `xhigh`, the route's top tier), llmux as `output_config.effort`, the field every llmux route reads it from; a model the catalog does not describe is sent none. **The speed** -- Fast and Ultra, exclusive, both off whenever the model changes by a query, and held to what the row offers by a pick -- is saved as `fast_mode`, `ultrafast_mode` and `fast_mode_model_bound` (profile-only) beside every model and speed change, and sent by the TUI on the Gateway as upstream's `providerOptions`: `{"gateway":{"speed":"fast"}}` for Fast on a row that offers it, `{"gateway":{"only":["openai"]},"openai":{"serviceTier":"ultrafast"}}` for Ultra -- which, with no silent downgrade, refuses the turn unless the provider is the Gateway, the id is `openai/…` and the row offers it. **llmux reads no per-request speed** (its Fast is the daemon's own codex setting, `2lab-ai/llmux src/config/schema.rs:545-550`, and its catalog publishes no speed), so no llmux row offers one, the speed column never opens there, and nothing is sent. The announcement is upstream's `* Switched to <id> (effort: <level or default>, speed: <normal|fast|ultrafast>)` -- `fast` also for an id ending `-fast` -- or `* Next turn will use …` while a turn runs or waits; the hint row's compact label follows the model in force, with the level after it when the model takes it, then `⚡︎` -- in the row's grey for Fast (bound to the model, or an id ending `-fast`), in upstream's yellow (`38;2;255;204;0`, `38;5;220` at 256 colours) for Ultra. **A change is a control message**, never refused for a full queue: while a turn runs -- or a permission or model question waits, which it does not answer -- it is put aside and applied the moment that turn ends, before anything queued behind it. One put aside while `/provider` switched is saved for the provider it was chosen under and lands on no selector of the new one (upstream refuses the switch while work runs, so its end state is the same), with a warning that says so. A failed catalog load says upstream's `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.` (or `AI Gateway rate limited model discovery; xfx retries automatically.`) where asking again could help -- upstream's words since 14a12fea, its own name read as xfx's -- and `Unable to load models.` otherwise (`retryableFailureText`, `model_menu_presentation.zig:435-443@481104fd`); either is read again past its hold. Upstream's third retryable sentence, `Could not refresh model catalog; xfx retries automatically.` in xfx's name, is for any other failure asking again could change: a catalog read that could be given no thread, or no runtime to run on (upstream's `.runtime`, `model_cache_runtime.zig:537-546`), told the moment it is found, a turn running or not, and read again past its hold like any other. A read past its deadline is upstream's retryable `.transport` (`catalogRequestFailure`, `builtins/gateway.zig:2510-2519`, its test `:2545-2547`) -- `Could not reach AI Gateway; xfx retries automatically.` -- which xfx says for a read whose send times out, or whose body is still coming at the deadline (`GatewayCatalog::fetch`, CATALOG-BOUNDS). The choice is saved as `models.<provider>` in `~/.xfx/settings.json` -- with `provider`, the speed keys and, for a pick (a menu row, the column, the explicit form, a `C-p` row, `/settings`' model list) of a model whose row lists levels, `effort` -- the one it chose or the session's -- as upstream's `selectModelFromPicker` writes them (`session_commands.zig:886-915`; a query writes no effort, `setResolvedModel`, `:1172-1188`) -- on the runtime thread, through the profile's staged `0600` writer, every other key kept, and read back at the next launch under the usual precedence (`XFX_MODEL` over the profile over the compiled default); when a higher layer will still decide a fresh session's model it says `* model: saved to user settings (scope=user); fresh sessions here use higher-precedence model=<layer>` -- `model picker:` for a change made in a column, the menu or the explicit form -- and a failed write `✗ model: active for this process but not saved to user settings (…)`. Every settings write -- a preference, and `xfx setup`'s, which merges the selection it decided into the file as it stands under the lock -- is one read-modify-write under an in-process lock and upstream's cross-process `settings.lock` beside the file, so `/statusline`, `/model` and a setup writing at once all survive. A durable `preferences_changed` event records the model, and the effort and both switches with every change to them -- and at the session's first save, which is the first prompt or a `/rename` before it, the ones in force, right behind `session_started`: a change made before then had no log to go into, and upstream holds every setting made before the first turn and writes them with it (`hold`, `writeFirstTurn`, `src/core/session_manager/session.zig:415-429`, `:503-539@481104fd`) -- and a resume -- at launch or from the picker -- puts the session's effort and speed back, as upstream's `restoreRuntimePreferences` does. **The line-oriented shell** keeps its own report: bare, the model, the provider and the layer that chose it, and the provider's catalog in the order it was published, bounded at 100 rows with an explicit `... and N more` line; with an argument the same resolution against a catalog it has loaded (an id it does not list is applied, not refused), the shared caveat (`provider::model::unverified_notice`) when no catalog could be consulted, and no profile write. **What a model id may be is one question with one answer on both surfaces**: `provider::model::model_id_problem` -- one printable word of at most 200 bytes, because the id becomes an HTTP header and a session-log field -- asked before the runtime is told anything. Upstream's Ctrl-P model picker is not here. The standalone `models` and `provider` commands remain separate deferred rows. `commands.zig:452`, `model_menu_presentation.zig`, `session_commands.zig:1147-1279`. |
 | `/effort` | slash | implemented | fx v0.0.16's command (`vercel-labs/fx@481104fd`, commit `04a5e3af`): upstream's row, "choose the reasoning effort for the current model", Model, after `/model` (`commands.zig:459`). **The TUI** answers it as upstream does. While a turn is running or a prompt is waiting for one -- upstream's busy rule; a catalog load, a `/model` change or another switch in the queue does not count -- the command says `* provider: Provider switching is unavailable until active and queued work finishes.` and opens nothing, and a column opened by typing refuses its Return the same way and keeps the text (`app_auth_runtime.zig:30`, `:430-439`). `/setup llmux` and a Return still switch at once, column or no column. Upstream's later columns -- the sign-in method, the Vercel team, the key source, the masked key field -- and its Codex and Grok rows are fx sign-in, gated (zbrain open-loop #88), and absent; the sentence on the row still names how a provider signs in because it is upstream's. **The line-oriented shell** keeps `/provider <gateway|llmux>` (or `/setup …`) as a complete command and says how to use it when it is given none. Both run the same decision procedure and write the same bytes as the `setup` subcommand, because all three are `provider::setup::prepare` followed by `provider::setup::commit` -- one decision procedure, one writer. **That shared pair is all the `setup` subcommand and the line shell perform**: they prepare, commit and re-read the configuration, and they do **not** run the snapshot/classify/rollback transaction described next, which is the TUI worker's alone. In the TUI the whole of it is a transaction on the runtime thread: the profile's preimage is snapshotted first, every network and read step runs before anything is written, a cancellation may win **only** before the commit, a commit error is classified by rereading the raw bytes rather than by trusting the error value, the configuration is then re-read from the file that was written, and a reload failure rolls back only while the disk still holds exactly the document this transaction wrote -- bytes and Unix mode both. Bytes it did not write are never overwritten: a third state on disk means another writer owns the file, and the session ends rather than clobbering it. What the shell shows afterwards -- the model, the credential fact -- is what the reload resolved to rather than what the write intended, so a settings layer that outranks the profile is named instead of silently winning. A switch drops the bundle -- a connection to an endpoint no longer configured -- and **keeps the conversation**, as upstream's `/provider` keeps its session (`app_auth_runtime.zig:1377-1381`): its log, grants, read proofs, `/undo` journal, title and the meter's tokens go on; the switch is written into the log as `preferences_changed` with the provider and the model (`commitRuntimePreferences`, `app_session_runtime.zig:3539-3550`); and the new provider's catalog is read at once, in the background as at launch (the `/model` row), as upstream's switch adopts the target's (`:1377`), so the meter measures the same tokens against the new window. The next request carries every earlier turn in the new provider's wire, rebuilt from the log: text, tool calls, results (a refusal as `execution-denied` on the Gateway and `is_error` on llmux), images, steers and a compaction checkpoint all translate. A step's sealed reasoning -- llmux's signed `thinking` blocks -- goes only to the provider **and the model** that sealed it, so a `/model` change on llmux drops it too, and the rest of the step goes as it is: its text and its calls (upstream's `ProviderReplay.matches` and `projectProviderReplay`, `types.zig:1512-1515`, `:1548-1563`); upstream drops it without a word, xfx says so once per conversation (`! session: this session recorded reasoning on the anthropic_messages wire and is continuing on vercel_gateway, so that reasoning was not carried over` after a switch, `! session: this session recorded reasoning from <model> and is continuing with <model>, so that reasoning was not carried over` after a model change). **It is said once it is handed to the screen:** a turn stopped before its request -- in its catalog wait (the `/model` row) -- hands nothing on and sends nothing, so the next turn, which sends the history, says it. The screen paints every notice it is handed, a stopped turn's too; a reset and a resume's or a fork's replay drop one, and with it the conversation whose ledger marked it, so a later conversation of the same session says it again. Upstream's lines said once a session are its `[context]` notices (`claimContextNotice`, `session.zig:1551-1561@481104fd`), each claimed where it is said: a project file's at submission, written to the transcript in the same step (`refreshProjectContext`, `app_agent_runtime.zig:906-938`, from `main.zig:1200`), which `processQueuedPrompt` then finds said (`:976-996`); the skill catalog's and the MCP catalog's only once the turn is past its stop checks (`orchestrator.zig:5950`, `:6001`, after `:5893-5948`; `app_callbacks.zig:1294-1307`, `app_agent_runtime.zig:852-867`), and claimed when they are queued rather than painted; skill diagnostics at launch and by `/skills`, written in the same step (`app_bootstrap_runtime.zig:292-308`, `app_commands.zig:1083-1098`). So upstream loses none of them to a stop before the request either. It does lose a catalog line whose turn is stopped after the claim and before the worker queue is drained, as the cancelled drain drops a semantic notice (`pushSemanticNotice`, `app_worker_runtime.zig:649-651`; the drain, `:922-950`; its drop arm, `:344-354`); xfx does not copy that loss. A call id the wire cannot take -- the Gateway relays ids such as `functions.read:0`, which Anthropic's `tool_use` refuses -- is sent under upstream's alias, `fx_` and 40 hex of its SHA-256 and a step (`tool_call_ids.zig`), on both wires as upstream does; the log keeps the id that was minted. A resume in the TUI -- `--resume` or `/resume` -- continues on the provider the session last recorded, whatever the profile says by then, in the model it last ran under there unless `XFX_MODEL` names one, and replays every turn in that provider's wire (upstream's `restoreRuntimePreferences`, `app_session_runtime.zig:6147`); when that provider has nothing to authenticate with, the session stays on it, says `! auth: <the turn's refusal>` once under the replay and refuses the next prompt with the same words (`restoreSessionCredential`, `app_auth_runtime.zig:172-182`) -- on any resume, the provider the session recorded or the one in force, as upstream says it on any. A log that recorded no provider resumes on the configured one; `xfx ask --resume` and the line shell still resume on the configured provider. The line shell's `/provider <gateway|llmux>` keeps the conversation the same way and says `[shell] provider=<p> model=<m>`. `commands.zig:472`, `app_commands.zig:734-745`. |

A rule's key is the exact action -- a command's text, directory, mode and platform; a change's tool, typed arguments, the identity of the directory it is made in, canonical target, preimage (device, inode, digest), the digest of what it would leave, and every path a configured rule judges it at -- so it matches that action and no other. Nothing is written before then. The first save -- the first prompt, or a `/rename` before one -- writes the whole held state into the new session's directory **before its log exists** -- refusing anything already at the store's path, a FIFO or a directory as much as a file, publishing with `link(2)` so one another writer puts there after that look is refused and left in place rather than replaced, and reading what it wrote back through the regular-file open every decision reads by -- so no session is ever seen without its rules, as upstream's first turn writes its held lines in the one publish that makes it visible (`hold`, `writeFirstTurn`, `src/core/session_manager/session.zig:415-429`, `:503-539@481104fd`); that turn's first call is decided by them. Refusals in upstream's words: no saved session yet -- the line shell's, whose session opens at its first prompt and holds nothing before it (`✗ permissions: saved-session permission rules require an active saved session`) -- a question up (`! … finish the active permission prompt before managing saved-session rules`), an action that cannot be prepared (`✗ permissions: could not prepare that exact permission action without executing it`), an unknown id (`! permissions: saved-session permission rule not found`), a malformed form (the two-row `✗ usage: /permissions remember …`). **Divergences:** the line shell refuses `remember` and `revoke` before its first prompt, where upstream takes them: this delta ported the TUI's lifecycle (PERM-EARLY), not the line shell's, whose session opens there. A read's rule is applied on both.  | `/allowlist` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row: `/allowlist [view [effective\|local\|user]\|[local\|user] add\|remove\|reset ...]`, "manage trusted commands, tools, and URLs", Security (`commands.zig:480`), every word case-insensitive (`handleAllowlist`, `session_commands.zig:396-444`). **Two tables, both in `~/.xfx/settings.json`**: `user` is the top-level `permission`, `local` (the default) is `workspaces["<canonical workspace>"].permission` -- never a file in the repository; `permission` is profile-only, so a `.xfx.json` that carries one is reported and ignored. -| full-screen TUI | ui | partial | Opt-in and narrower than upstream's. `XFX_TUI=1` on a bare `xfx` takes the terminal into raw mode on its **normal** buffer: the band, the transcript and the composer live there for the life of the session and never move, and mouse reporting is never enabled, so the terminal's own scrollback and wheel keep working. The alternate screen is an owned excursion and nothing else -- `\x1b[?1049h` is written only to review a change too big for the band to show, for as long as that question is unanswered (below), and for the full transcript `C-o` opens, for as long as it is open (its own row below); a session that does neither, however long, writes it never. Under tmux the kitty keyboard push is omitted, because sending it there breaks key input. Raw mode is an obligation rather than a feature: the entry bits (`ECHO`/`ICANON`/`IEXTEN`/`ISIG` clear, `CS8`, `VMIN=1`, `VTIME=0`) are asserted positively while the child runs, and the `termios` captured before entry is put back on exit -- onto the descriptor it was captured from, and whether or not the screen could still be written. The band is a divider, the composer, and a hint row at the bottom of the screen -- with one more row above the divider while a turn is running, saying what it is doing, and a block of rows between the two while that turn is waiting to be told whether it may change something; every frame is one write wrapped in synchronized output (`?2026h`/`l`) -- a frame that also grows the document carries those rows inside the same update, ahead of the band, as upstream's frame carries its movement bytes (`composeWireFrame`, `src/ui/render_engine/terminal_diff.zig:604-645` at `c1db919`), so a terminal that honours the mode never presents a new row with the band still where the scroll left it -- and what that write carries is the **difference** between what the terminal is holding and what it should be holding: the band keeps a shadow grid of grapheme cells, wide-cell continuations and attribute state, builds the grid the frame would leave, and emits a `CUP`, replacement text and an `EL` for the rows that changed and for nothing else -- so a keystroke costs the cell it changed and an animation phase costs the digit it turned over. **A frame whose cells, window title and caret are all unchanged writes zero bytes**, which is what keeps an idle session silent between the loop's 8 ms ticks. The shadow is advanced only by a write that succeeded, so a refused frame is owed again rather than believed; a Ctrl-L and the resume after a `SIGTSTP` mark the screen externally damaged, and the frame after either opens with the Phase-1 erase -- the rows a shrinking band gave back, then `CUP` to the band's top row and `ED` -- because a forgotten shadow is *unknown* rather than blank, and a diff that treated it as blank would rewrite the band's own columns and leave whatever the shell put beside them on every band row. The erase starts at the band's top row and never above it, so the terminal's own document survives a resume. A resume also re-arms the window title: the restore that hands the terminal back pops the title stack, so the title the session set is gone, and the band owes a fresh `OSC 2` -- one push per time the terminal is taken and one pop per time it is given back, so a stop/resume cycle leaves the stack balanced. The optimization is judged by the screen it leaves rather than by the bytes it saves: the `fault-injection` build keeps the Phase-1 whole-band painter behind a compile-time seam (`Fault::FullPaintReference`, absent from every released binary), and `scripts/smoke-tui.sh`'s scenario 13 drives identical facts through both painters on a real pseudoterminal and requires the final cells, attributes and caret to be equal. **The terminal's window title is set** -- `OSC 2 ; xfx · <model>`, with every control stripped out of the model label so a configured name cannot close the sequence and have the rest of itself executed -- and it is borrowed rather than taken: the mode set pushes the terminal's own title onto its stack (`CSI 22 ; 2 t`) and every restore path, normal and abnormal, pops it back (`CSI 23 ; 2 t`). A title is written as its own `OSC 2` right ahead of the frame's synchronized update and never inside it, which is where upstream's lands: upstream writes a title the moment it changes, as a write of its own between frames (`syncTerminalTitleWith`, `src/core/app/app_session_runtime.zig:3608-3629`; `setTerminalTitleLabel`, `src/ui/render.zig:723-733` at `c1db919`), so its bytes reach the terminal after one frame's `?2026l` and before the next one's `?2026h`; the output check refuses an update led by anything else, or by a title other than the one the band owes. The line-oriented shell sets no title and pushes nothing. **What has already reached the terminal's own scrollback is never rewritten**: the diff writes only at or below the band's top row, plus the rows a shrinking band gave back, and a document append still scrolls the screen with real linefeeds -- committed rows stay the terminal's. A terminal smaller than 6 rows or 20 columns is refused with a reason rather than painted over. **What those rows say**: the divider spans the screen, the composer shows what has been typed into it behind its prompt marker, and the hint row says what a turn would be run with -- in upstream's order, joined by upstream's separator: a call to action when the configured provider has no usable credential (a Gateway with no bearer token or a llmux with no URL -- both refuse every turn, and the row says so before a prompt is spent on one), the permission mode, and a compact model label that drops the provider prefix and the `claude-` family prefix. A refusal takes the left of it whole when one has just happened, and `esc again to clear` sits flush with the last column while the double-Esc gesture is armed, the row's colours closed before its padding as upstream's are (`input_presentation.zig:551-567`). On a screen too narrow for all of it the segments are **dropped whole, from the right**, rather than clipped mid-number: the permission mode gives way in front of the model rather than the model behind it, and the right-hand warning is reserved before the left is measured, because it is about a key that is already armed. The meter upstream ends that row with is here too and is filled from a completed turn's own `input_tokens` over the context window the provider's catalog publishes, shown only when the session has both numbers and dropped whole rather than defaulted when it has one; what is still absent is the shortcut legend. **While a turn is in flight there is one row above the divider**, and only then: it says `• Thinking` with how long the turn has been running, or names the tool that is running instead of saying `Thinking` at all -- a turn that has gone quiet because a tool is taking a minute otherwise looks exactly like a turn that has gone quiet. The marker blinks every 500 ms, counted in the renderer's own 50 ms animation phases rather than on a second clock beside it, and the row asks for a frame when its text changes rather than on every phase, so a band whose row says the same thing is not repainted twenty times a second and an idle band asks for nothing at all. The row is the **band's** while it exists: it takes its row from the bottom of the document exactly as a composer that grew by one does, and the divider, the composer and the caret do not move when a turn starts. It is **exactly the turn's lifetime**: the runtime says when a turn begins and says when it ends, in order on the one channel that carries everything else it says, and the band draws the row between the two. Told rather than counted, and that is correctness rather than taste -- a prompt is accepted the moment it is typed and may then wait behind another turn for a minute (the hint row is where waiting is said, and a waiting prompt gets no row), and the place a finished turn holds is given back *after* its conclusion is sent, so a band counting places would announce a turn that had not started and start a clock for one that no longer exists. **The clock is one turn's**: a prompt queued behind a running one does not restart it, and when that turn ends the row begins again from zero for whatever turn the runtime starts next rather than carrying the finished one's elapsed time forward. A `/model` travels on the same channel but is not a turn, and no row appears for one. Two things named rather than implied: the elapsed clock **stops while xfx is waiting for a decision**, because that interval measures the person rather than the model -- the approval panel below is what pends one, and the row itself is off the band while a question is up, as upstream's is (`render_input.zig:705`), and comes back at the elapsed time it stopped at; and the token count upstream shows beside the elapsed time is **absent**, because the streamed event set carries no usage -- the row says nothing about tokens rather than reporting a zero nobody measured. What is *above* the divider is the turn, described below. The band's size is asked of **standard output**, because that is the screen it is drawn on; raw mode is entered on standard input, because that is where input arrives; and a redirected session whose two ends are different terminals gets no cursor report at all, so it starts at row 1 and pushes nothing. A screen that refuses every frame for half a second of wall-clock time ends the session with the first of those errors, on a terminal the exit still puts back, rather than being retried invisibly forever; the budget is measured on a clock rather than in repaints, because the 8 ms tick is the longest a turn waits and not the shortest. The exit clears from the band's top row downward (`app_lifecycle.zig:578-593`), and only when a band was really painted: a session that left before its first frame -- a Ctrl-D that arrived with the cursor report -- moves no cursor and erases nothing. At launch it asks the terminal where the cursor is (CSI `6n`, 100 ms deadline, row 1 if the terminal does not answer; the row under it when the cursor is past the first column) and pushes the shell output above that row into scrollback -- or, with `/settings`' `Startup scrollback` off, starts on that row under it and pushes only what leaves fewer than eleven document rows (`app_lifecycle.zig:944-988`) -- moving the cursor to the bottom row first, because a linefeed scrolls a terminal only from the bottom margin and from anywhere else merely walks the cursor down -- so the band never paints over what was already on screen; a keystroke typed while the query is in flight is deferred, not swallowed. Restoration is proven one case per exit path, each on a real pty: the normal exit, `SIGTERM`/`SIGHUP`/an external `SIGINT` (the child dies *by the signal*, which is what proves the handler reset the disposition and re-raised rather than fabricating a status) -- **except a `SIGINT` the process was started ignoring** (`nohup … &`), which stays ignored, so a `kill -INT` changes nothing and every command the session runs inherits the ignore, as upstream's TUI catches no `SIGINT` at all (`installAbnormalExitHandlers` owns `SIGTERM` and `SIGHUP` only, `app_lifecycle.zig:82-104` at `c1db9191`) -- and `SIGTSTP`/`SIGCONT` including a **second** `SIGTSTP` after a resume, which is what proves the stop handler was reinstalled. Every one of those is an ordinary transition rather than a race, because the owned signals are blocked across the whole of it -- from before the `tcsetattr` that takes the terminal until after the last handler is installed -- so a supervisor that signals xfx during startup finds a handler rather than a default disposition and a raw terminal. The signals named here are the ones a *supervisor or an operator* sends: with `ISIG` cleared a **typed** Ctrl-C or Ctrl-Z generates no signal at all, and arrives as a byte for the input decoder. Ctrl-C is a byte, not a signal, and it means what upstream's semantic Ctrl-C means (`app_input_runtime.zig:1675-1712` at `c1db919`): over a running turn with a draft in the composer it takes the draft and nothing else; otherwise every press is a press of one three-second exit window -- the first arms it, says `press ctrl+c again to exit` on the hint row for as long as it lasts (the `/help` catalog's row included), and stops the running turn or throws the idle draft away; a second inside it leaves with **130**, disjoint from the external `SIGINT` above, which still kills the process by the signal unless the launcher ignored it; any other keystroke closes the window early. A stopped turn leaves upstream's line, `▪ Cancelled · What can xfx do differently?`, at the keystroke: the answer stops where it was on the screen (the line it had not finished is not painted, nor is anything still crossing from the runtime) and no `✗ system:` or summary follows; a running command is killed at the keystroke with its process group, its row `└ Cancelled <command>` and its line `▪ Cancelled <command> · …` the turn's (the UI raises the turn's own flag, which the command's supervisor polls), where a running file call runs to its end first -- **and the interrupt drops whatever was queued behind it**, said as `⊘ queue: dropping what was queued behind it as well.`, because a queue that started running a moment after the user asked everything to stop would be the surprise the band's `┋` row is there to prevent; a prompt typed *after* the interrupt is a new intention and still runs, which the interrupt is told the boundary of rather than left to guess. What the model is told about the stopped turn is upstream's: the cut-off answer closed with `The previous response ended before completion.` (or the tools that finished, named), a call the turn stopped under -- one that never ran, a command the user stopped, or a question the user cancelled -- answered `aborted by user` as a failure, then the `<turn_aborted>` user message (`session.zig:52-64`, `:2695-2747`); any failed tool result is sent as a failure (`error-text` on the Gateway, `is_error` on llmux; `vercel_protocol.zig:742-747`), and a refusal as `execution-denied` with the refusal as its reason (`vercel_protocol.zig:742-744`; `is_error` on llmux, which has no word of its own). A typed Ctrl-Z still binds nothing -- upstream's suspends (`app_input_runtime.zig:862-866@481104fd`), so here only a `SIGTSTP` stops the session; owner unit: **CTRL-Z** (queued) -- and Ctrl-D is not an exit while a turn is running. Escape is a two-tap: over a running turn a second one within a second stops it, and the hint row says `esc again to interrupt` (narrower: `esc esc interrupt`, `esc esc`) in between; at an idle prompt with a draft a second one within 500 ms clears the composer, and the row says `esc again to clear`, because nothing else in this phase discards a draft without a key that says so; at an empty idle composer Escape arms nothing (`app_input_runtime.zig:3210-3213`). A prompt submitted while a turn is running is upstream's steer, delivered into that turn at upstream's boundaries (`worker_runtime.zig:1080-1104`, `orchestrator.zig:157-261`): over a tool-free answer at once -- the answer cut where it had got to and kept as the assistant's message -- while a tool runs after its results, and with the model's answer after it, the turn going on; the model is told each in upstream's `<user_steering>` wrapper and a later turn replays the words as typed. It is the band's `┋ <prompt>` row in the dim grey until the turn takes it and its card is written then; Up at an empty composer takes the newest back while it still waits for a tool (`popQueuedSteerForEdit`); a stop drops steers with the queue, and one the turn never took runs as the next prompt. A request the turn retries (a status the server says is transient, a connection that never opened) replaces the activity row with upstream's recovery label, `⚠ Provider unavailable · HTTP 500 · server_error: upstream exploded · retrying request in 1s`, counting down to the next attempt and kept through a retried answer arriving, then `✓ recovered · attempt N` for 1.5 s or until the turn ends (`worker_status.zig:5`, `:221-235`), a label too wide for the screen wrapping onto up to three rows under its mark (`render_input.zig:406`, `:689-697`); the retry budget is xfx's own three attempts, not upstream's (re-verified against fx v0.0.16, `481104fd`, which changes nothing a retry shows: upstream's label, `recoveryLabel`, `src/core/shared/types.zig:539-606@481104fd`, differs from 0.0.13's only by an action the retry path never takes, and its count still rounds up, `format_retry_label`, `src/core/output/worker_status.zig:201-219`, `:212@481104fd` -- xfx's `src/tui/retry.rs:369-381`, `:384-391`, with its three causes at `:252-261`; the statuses retried are still 429, 500, 502, 503 and 504, `fromHttpStatus`, `src/core/agent/stream_provider.zig:480-492`, and `isRetryableModelFailure`, `src/core/agent/runtime/orchestrator.zig:4496-4501@481104fd`, as xfx's `src/gateway/mod.rs:994-995`; 0.0.16's one change on that path, `fe7925b2`, deletes a stall stop a status failure could never reach; and upstream's turn still never ends on a transient failure, backing off 250 ms, 1 s, 2 s and on to 30 s and slowing to once a minute after fifteen minutes, `src/core/agent/runtime/model_response_recovery.zig:5-9`, `:270-282@481104fd`, where xfx's three attempts wait 250 and then 500 ms, `src/gateway/mod.rs:59`, `src/agent/machine.rs:78-85`, `:199-207`), and Escape over a retry arms the interrupt because upstream pauses only its unbudgeted wait for connectivity, which xfx does not have. That decoder is the session's own: bytes in, keystrokes out, one machine fed in arrival order (the keystrokes the launch probe read with the cursor report included), with a lone `ESC` resolved as the Escape key only after 50 ms of quiet, an unfinished `CSI` waited for however long the terminal takes, an unknown sequence bounded at 32 bytes and reported as a keystroke with no binding rather than as a phantom Escape, `ESC` followed by a control byte delivered as the Escape key *and* that byte -- except `ESC DEL`, which is Alt-Backspace and deletes back over one word of word characters, as upstream's does (`composer_deletion.zig:103-136`) where `C-w` deletes back to whitespace, and `ESC CR`/`ESC LF`, which is Alt-Enter and inserts a newline (`escape_parser.zig` at `c1db919`, :515-519), because replaying either would make two of them two Escapes inside the double-Esc window and throw the whole draft away, and an Alt-Enter a submit besides -- and **no** C0 byte or control scalar ever delivered as text -- a tab included, because a control that reached the composer would reach the terminal again on submit; a tab is a *named keystroke* instead, which the approval panel walks its choices with and the composer has no binding for. **Key reports are decoded in both of their spellings** -- kitty's `CSI <key> ; <mods> u` and `modifyOtherKeys`' `CSI 27 ; <mods> ; <key> ~` -- through one port of upstream's `kittyUnicodeKeyAction` (`escape_parser.zig` at `c1db919`, :185-266), in its branch order: a Ctrl letter is that letter's control byte and therefore its binding, Escape, Enter with a modifier, Alt-Backspace, Alt-b/f, Super+Z and the keypad are the keys they already are here, Caps Lock and Num Lock are not modifiers, and a key **release** is never a keystroke; Super+A, Super+C and Super+X are the composer's selection (:223-231) -- select the whole draft, copy it, cut it, through the clipboard `/copy` uses, the copy silent and the cut taking the text out only once the clipboard has it (`cutSelection`, `input_selection_runtime.zig:32-42`), the selected run painted in reverse video (`input_presentation.zig:995-1021`); Alt-d deletes the word to the right; Ctrl+O opens the full transcript; and a page key, bare or held with a modifier (`pageKeyAction`, :169-173), pages whichever surface has the keys -- the full transcript while it is open, the list of an open menu or picker by the composer's row cap (`routeHelpMenuMove`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:449-466`), and otherwise the composer's caret by the same number of rows. What upstream resolves to a key xfx still does not have -- a selection-extending move, spelled on a letter (Ctrl+Shift+A/B/E/F, Alt+Shift+B/F) or with Shift on a page key, and the all-sessions toggle (`open_all_sessions`, :253) -- binds nothing beyond the plain move; Shift-Tab, upstream's `toggle_permission_mode` (:254), is the permission-mode cycle (the `/permissions` row). A selection-extending **arrow** is the exception and is not a dead key: kitty's Up and Down (`57352`/`57353`) and the keypad's arrows, Home and End resolve through xfx's own cursor table, which drops Shift together with the selection it would have extended, so with Shift held -- alone or beside Ctrl or Alt -- the key makes the plain move it makes here unshifted (a word move for Left and Right under Ctrl or Alt) where upstream would extend a selection; the legacy `CSI 1 ; <mod> A` spelling takes the same table. Every key herdr 0.9.3 (libghostty-vt, kitty flag 1) was captured sending in that form decodes to its binding, and its `u`-form Ctrl-U and Ctrl-D are driven end to end on a real pty (`.prd/tui-phase3/receipts/2026-10-06-herdr-csi-u-keys.md`). The grammar is stricter than upstream's -- one canonical decimal spelling per field, no extra field -- and not looser anywhere. Bracketed paste is framed: `?2004h` is enabled, the bytes between `ESC[200~` and `ESC[201~` are content rather than keys -- so a newline inside a paste does not submit it, a `0x03` does not cancel a turn, and an escape sequence is not obeyed -- and anything over 1000 codepoints is shown in the composer as `[Pasted text #N, M lines]` (`1 line` for one) and expanded back into the prompt when it is sent -- the prompt's card, the turn's estimate and a steer's card are that expanded text, every line on rows of its own, as upstream's card is (`input_submit_runtime.zig:705-706`, `user_message_card.zig:348-383`, `orchestrator.zig:5482-5485`) -- expanded to the text the frame made of it, which is the pasted bytes filtered, decoded as UTF-8 with anything that is not UTF-8 replaced, and with CR and CRLF line breaks normalized to LF, rather than to the bytes the terminal sent. **A prompt's card is shown in the frame that clears the composer**, as fx v0.0.16's 70611a91 shows one (`submit` queues the card in the `route_bytes` that takes the draft, `src/tui/shell.rs` `send`/`echo`): whole, when it fits between the document's last row and fx's fixed content bottom, `rows - 4` (`footer_rows`, `src/main.zig:173@481104fd`; `p5-transcript-submit` wire, the first frame after Enter); otherwise only its newest rows -- as many as that room holds, at least one, and no more than the frame's band leaves under the row above the card and the blank row over the activity row -- at the band's top, and the card itself, whole, in the next frame, once that one has landed (`buildPendingCardProjection`, `PendingCardPaintContext`, `transcriptAreaBeforePendingTail`, `noteCommittedFrame`: `src/core/app/app_render_runtime.zig:168-277`, `:2230-2238`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:289-307@481104fd`; `Shell::preview_room`, `preview_card`, `preview_landed`). A three-line prompt on a full 40-row screen previews its last line, five lines their last three, a 60-line pasted block its last 32 rows under the welcome (`p5-transcript-submit-multi`, `p5-submit-preview-*` wires; `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/submit-card-preview/`). The preview is the band's rows, not the document's, so none of it reaches scrollback, and every screen after it is the one the card was written onto before. The card's frame follows the preview's in the same tick, with nothing read and no resize taken between them, as fx writes the card in its next frame once its preview's has committed (`noteCommittedFrame`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:289-307@481104fd`; `Shell::take_preview_shown`). What stays open is a write that fails, as it does between fx's two writes: the card's frame refused or torn before its rows, or the preview's frame torn, and then a screen that shrinks before the retry -- the preview's rows the terminal moves into its history, or keeps above the band where they are the document's to xfx after a resize, stay there, and the card is still written once, whole and in order (`a_failed_write_and_a_shrink_before_the_retry_leave_only_what_the_shrink_took`). The room is measured against the last landed frame, as fx measures from its last committed one (`src/ui/transcript/runtime.zig:9929-9932@481104fd`; five lines and their Enter in one read preview what the one-row composer's frame left, `p5-submit-preview-oneread`). Checked against fx at 40x120, 30x100 and 24x80.
